TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Richard M. Machesky, Ed.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 21, 2020 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON 
The first order of business is to e lect an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  
Past practice has been that the previous year’s president conducts this 
part of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of business is for the acting chairperson  to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temporary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT 
The acting chairperson declares nominations in order for the office o f 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not 
be seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the 
nomination.)  Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT 
The newly elected  president declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY 
The president declares nominations in order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER 
According to Board Policy, the treasurer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served  as treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue.

         4. C.        01.21.20 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to time, come into the 
hands of Rick West , Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies 
belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the State or 
otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to be 
designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that said Treasurer, Rick West, is hereby 
directed to deposit and/or invest all public monies, including tax monies now in or 
coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the  following 
depository institutions: 
Bank of America 
Charter One Bank 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Huntington Bank  
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, 
and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, or the 
Superintendent, Richard M. Machesky, who may sign on the General Fund account 
and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging t o the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The Troy 
Career Center Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the account 
established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and dependent 
care flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next Generation 
Enrollment, Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, 
Rick West, and 
11
         4. C.        01.21.20 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Rick West, may open or close 
accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of the 
District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested according 
to Board Policy 5000.06; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine money 
from more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment authorized by  
subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School Code; and 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code. 
I, ______________________, Secretary of Troy School District, Oakland County, 
Michigan, certify that the Resolution was adopted by said Board at a meeting 
thereof held on the date specified above. 
Given under my hand and seal of the Troy School District in the County of 
Oakland in the State of Michigan this 21st day of January 2020.

5. RECOGNITION 
A. TSD Mission Moment – Michigan Arctic Grayling Initiative  
This evening, the Board would like to recognize Adam Burns, Technology 
Education/English teacher at Athens High School and a student team from 
Athens TV for their work on the Michigan Arctic Grayling Initiative.  At this 
time, we would like to invite Mr. Burns up to introduce the team and talk 
about this initiative and what the students have been doing to help.   
Recommended Action:  Invite Adam Burns to the podium.   
Students  
Ellen Bedford 
Abby Bright 
Grace Bright 
Grace Joo 
Madison Strachan 
18
 5. B. 01.21.2020 
5. RECOGNITION 
B. 2020 National Excellence in Literacy Leadership Award 
This evening, the Board would like to recognize Kris Griffor, Assistant 
Superintendent, Elementary Instruction for receiving the 2020 National 
Excellence in Literacy Leadership Award.  This is an award that is presented 
by the Reading Recovery Teacher Lead ers across the country to an 
administrator that has displayed a strong commitment to expand and 
maintain the high standards of Reading Recovery and made significant 
contributions to implementation beyond the local level.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Invite Kris Griffor to the podium to be  
recognized.   
19
     5. D.   01.15.2020 
5.   RECOGNITION  
D. Years of Dedicated Service to the Troy Board of Education -  
                                                                                       Paula Fleming 
This evening, in the Recognition portion of our meeting, we would like to take a moment 
to recognize one of our own Board members - Paula Fleming who is retiring from the 
Troy Board of Education .  Paula and her family are beginning a new chapter in their 
lives and are in the process of moving to Arkansas.  This evening is Mrs. Fleming’s final 
meeting with the Troy Board of Education. It’s hard to describe the impact Mrs. Fleming 
has had on our community, but it has been tremendous. Paula is leaving us after 
dedicating more tha n 19 years of her time, talents and passion into learning for all  
members of our Troy School District community.  
Aside from being an elected position, m ost of you know that serving on the Board of 
Education is also a volunteer position, and it requires a great deal of time, effort and 
commitment. Mrs. Fleming is an exemplary example of this - she is a trustee who has 
done a remarkable service to past generations and future generations of Troy students. 
We know this is not goodbye forever…. but this Board, will truly miss her. 
Tonight, there are also two proclamations to be read in recognition of Mrs. Fleming. 
Recommended Action:  Welcome Mayor Ethan Baker and State 
Representative Padma Kuppa to the podium to read their proclamations.   
Present certificate.

In  addition  to  Mr.  Schmidt,  present  were  Board  members

It was moved by Dr. Wilson and supported by Dr. Philippart 
that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board of Education 
of Michigan (the “School District”), held in the School District, 
on December 17, 2019, at 7:00 p.m., prevailing Eastern time. 
WHEREAS, on September 17, 2019, the Board authorized the 
issuance  of  bonds  in  the  aggregate  principal  sum  of  not  to 
exceed  Six  Million  One  Hundred  Thousand  Dollars 
($6,100,000) (the “Bonds”) for the purposes of the acquisition 
of  real  property  and  a  building  located  thereon  and  the 
renovation,  equipping  and  furnishing  of  such  building  (the 
“Projects”); and 
WHEREAS, the School District intends at this time to state its 
intentions to be reimbursed from proceeds of the Bonds for 
any  expenditures  undertaken  by the  School  District  for  the 
Projects prior to issuance of the Bonds. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
1.    The School District makes the following declarations for 
the  purpose  of  complying  with  the  reimbursement  rules  of 
Treas. Reg. § 1.150‐2 pursuant to the Internal Revenue Code 
of 1986, as amended: 
(a)  The  School  District  reasonably  expects  to 
reimburse itself with proceeds of the Bonds for 
certain costs of the Projects which were paid or 
will  be  paid  from  general  funds  of  the  School 
District  subsequent  to  sixty  (60)  days  prior  to 
today.  
(b)  The maximum principal amount of the Bonds, 
including  issuance  costs,  is  $6,100,000.  The 
Bonds will fund the costs of the Projects. 
(c)  A  reimbursement  allocation  of  the  capital 
expenditures  described  above  with  the 
proceeds of the Bonds will occur not later than 
18  months  after  the  later  of  (i)  the  date  on 
which  the  expenditure  is  paid,  or  (ii)  the  date 
t h e  P r o j e c t s  a r e  p l a c e d  i n  s e r v i c e  o r  
abandoned, but in no event more than three (3) 
years after the original expenditure is paid.  A 
reimbursement  allocation  is  an  allocation  in 
writing that evidences the School District’s use 
of the proceeds of the Bonds to reimburse the 
School District for a capital expenditure made 
pursuant to this resolution. 
 4 December 17, 2019 
31
(d)  The  expenditures  described  in  (a)  above  are 
“capital expenditures” as defined in Treas. Reg. 
§ 1.1.150‐1(b),  which  are  any  costs  of  a  type 
which  are  properly  chargeable  to  a  capital 
a c c o u n t  ( o r  w o u l d  b e  s o  c h a r g e a b l e  w i t h  a  
proper  election  or  with  the  application  of  the 
definition of placed in service under Treas. Reg. 
§ 1.150‐2(c)) under general Federal income tax 
principles  (as  determined  at  the  time  the 
expenditure is paid). 
( e )   N o  p r o c e e d s  o f  t h e  b o r r o w i n g  p a i d  t o  t h e  
School  District  in  reimbursement  pursuant  to 
this  Resolution  will  be  used  in  a  manner 
described  in  Treas.  Re g.  § 1.150‐2(h)  with 
respect  to  abusive  uses  of  such  proceeds, 
including,  but  not  limited  to,  using  funds 
corresponding  to  the  proceeds  of  the 
b o r r o w i n g  i n  a  m a n n e r  t h a t  r e s u l t s  i n  t h e  
creation  of  replacement  proceeds  (within 
Treas.  Reg.  § 1.148‐1)  within  one  year  of  the 
reimbursement  allocation  described  in  (c) 
above. 
2.        All  resolutions  and  parts  of  resolutions  insofar  as  they 
conflict with the provisions of this resolution be and the same 
hereby are rescinded. 
RESOLUTION DECLARED ADOPTED.

A. CONSIDERATION:  National Equity Project Conference 
As required by Board Policy 1000.01, Organization and Functioning of the 
Board; Reimbursement of Expenses, “Any expenditure of District funds for 
members for out -of-state meetings must have prior approval by 
resolution.”  Board President Karl Schmidt  has indicated his desire to 
attend the National Equity Project Conference “Leading for Equity 
Residential”, which will be held i n Itasca, Illinois from February 26 - 29, 
2020. Therefore, the Board is being asked to consider the following 
resolution.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education takes action to approve Mr. 
Schmidt’s attendance as a representative of the Troy Board of Education at the 
National Equity Project Conference in Itasca, Illinois from February 26 - 29, 2020. 
YES:      
NO:      
140
FEB 
26 
Leading for Equity, Residential | February 26 -29, 2020 | IL  
by National Equity Project   
$1,800  
Date And Time  
Wed, Feb 26, 2020, 5:00 PM – 
Sat, Feb 29, 2020, 9:30 PM CST  
Add to Calendar  
Location  
Eaglewood Resort  
1401 Nordic Rd  
Itasca, IL 60143  
View Map  
Refund Policy  
Refunds up to  30 days  before event  
Description  
Our Leading For Equity Residential  institute provides ample opportunities for sharing, 
reflection, and planning. We develop individual and collective leadership at every level 
of schools, districts, foundations or nonprofit organizations to d evelop capacity to foster 
positive change on behalf of historically under -served students and families. Participants 
deepen their commitment, relationships, and efficacy while developing strategies 
toward equity goals. Teams receive expert, caring guidance  and facilitation to work 
together to address the personal and technical challenges they face in their educational 
equity efforts.  
We offer two versions of the Leading for Equity Institute – residential and 
nonresidential. This is a residential offering. T o see dates for nonresidential 
institutes,  click here . 
Our residential LFEs are four -day, three -night institutes held at beautiful retreat centers 
surrounde d by nature. They provide the space and extended time together for deep 
conversations and meaningful relationship -building and strengthening. This longer 
version of the institute includes additional content and expanded opportunities for both 
individual an d team reflection, dialogue and planning.  
141
Both residential and non -residential LFE institute offerings are designed for teams. As 
such, you must register with a minimum of two and a maximum of eight people per 
team.  If your team has more than eight people, we encourage you to split your team to 
attend different LFE institute dates. If you would like to discuss bringing a team of more 
than eight people, please contact us at  events@nationalequityproject.org , as we can 
occasionally accommodate larger teams, particularly at non -residential institutes.  
Important Lodging Information: 
Participants will be lodged in shared double rooms with separate beds with another member of 
their team or, occasionally, a member of a different team. We prepare rooming lists based on 
pronouns shared at the time of registration. If you have any concerns about lodging, please 
email events@nationalequityproject.org. Special accommodations are subject to availability 
and are first come, first served. We do our best to accommodate special requests but may not 
be able to meet every request. We thank you for your understanding! 
Registration Payment Information: 
Our institutes have very limited capacity to ensure a great experience for participants. As such, 
they often sell out well in advance. Don't delay securing your registration! The quickest and 
easiest way to pay your registration fee and secure your spot is by credit card right here on 
Eventbrite. 
If you need to pay by check or purchase order, please complete a check registration form and 
send with check made payable to: National Equity Project, 1720 Broadway, 4th Floor, Oakland, 
CA 94612. 
If you need an invoice generated in order to process payment, please complete check 
registration form and email to events@nationalequityproject.org and we'll return an invoice to 
you. 
Cancellations and Registration Transfers: 
For this institute, we offer a refund for registrants who cancel at least 3 weeks prior to the 
event start date. In the event that you register and do not attend, you are still responsible for 
full payment. You can request a refund directly from Eventbrite or by 
emailing events@nationalequityproject.org. 
We allow registration to be transferred to a different attendee up to 3 weeks prior to the event. 
To request a registration transfer, email events@nationalequityproject.org.
